It’s the money, the family business… she’s been working her way into it, and then I’m the one left out when decisions get made.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “Oh, that’s just family, don’t make a fuss,” but that’s how things get covered up. My cousin had a situation like that years ago with some land, and everybody smiled until the papers were signed. So, no, I don’t think it would stop just because I wasn’t involved. I came here because I’ve been seeing this for over a year, and I know what she’s doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister is something that might not be true?\n\nPatient: I mean… people keep asking me that like I’m supposed to say maybe I imagined the whole thing. But no, I don’t think so. I came here because my husband’s sister has been working that family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions.\n\nAnd you know, families will smile and say, “Oh, don’t worry about it,” like when my aunt used to say nobody talks about money until somebody’s already taken it. But I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on more than a year, no matter how folks try to smooth it over.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever caught your husband's sister doing something that directly supports your belief that she is trying to push you out of decisions?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough, yes. I mean, I may not have some paper in my hand with her name circled on it, but I came here because she’s been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions.\n\nLike, people in the family act like I’m making too much of it—same way folks always say, “Oh, just keep peace,” and then later everybody’s surprised when somebody got left with nothing. But no, I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your husband's sister was trying to push you out of decisions?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, before all this started, I wasn’t thinking that way about her every day. It really began around the time we started having those arguments over the family care planning.\n\nThat’s been about… a year and two months now, I’d say. And once it started, it just kept showing up in how she acted. You know, families say it’s “just planning,” but planning is where people get moved aside if you’re not watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your husband's sister would change if everyone in your family agreed that she is not trying to push you out of decisions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don’t think it would change just because everybody agreed on saying that. Families agree on things all the time when they don’t want trouble, you know? My mother used to say people will sit right at the same table and pretend the soup ain’t burning.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working that family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. And no matter how folks try to smooth it over, I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been more than a year now, so everybody saying “no, no, that’s not happening” wouldn’t just erase it for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your husband's sister might not be trying to push you out of decisions, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: For a moment? I mean… people ask me that, like maybe I’m supposed to say, “Well maybe she’s just helping,” but that’s how folks get fooled. My aunt used to let everybody “help” with her papers and next thing you know nobody knew where anything went.\n\nSo no, not really. I came because my husband’s sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of decisions. People can smooth it over and call it misunderstanding, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on more than a year now.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't think your husband's sister was trying to push you out of decisions for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I can’t say there was a few weeks like that, not since it started. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, ever since those arguments with my husband’s sister over the family care planning.\n\nI mean, before all that, sure, I wasn’t sitting around thinking on her like this. I had other things on my mind—house stuff, bills, my neighbor’s dog always getting loose—but once those planning arguments started, that’s when I began seeing what she was doing, and it hasn’t really let up.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your husband's sister is trying to push you out of decisions ever completely gone away, even if just for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it ever completely went away. Since it started, it’s been there.\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months now, ever since them arguments with my husband’s sister over the family care planning started. And you know how family planning gets—everybody acting like they’re just “trying to help,” but then papers get moved around and people start talking over you. So no, not even for a little while, not really.\n\nDoctor: How does your husband's sister plan to benefit from pushing you out of the family business decisions?\n\nPatient: She benefits by getting control of the money, that’s the whole thing. If I’m pushed out of the family business decisions, then she can say, “Oh, we already handled that,” or “You weren’t part of that,” and then I’m just standing there like I don’t count.\n\nAnd people act like money stuff is just paperwork, but it’s not. My cousin went through something like that with a bank account once, everybody smiling and saying it was all for convenience, and then she couldn’t get a straight answer from nobody. That’s how it starts.\n\nSo yes, I think my husband’s sister has been working it so she can take over the money side and make decisions without me. Folks can try to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your husband's sister is trying to push you out of decisions changed at all since it first started?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. If anything, I just got clearer about it after I started noticing how she was working things in the family business, you know, getting herself in the middle of the money and decisions.\n\nPeople keep trying to smooth it over, like, “Oh, she didn’t mean it that way,” but I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s been more than a year now, and it hasn’t changed much. She’s trying to push me out so she can have control.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility, no matter how small, that your husband's sister is not trying to push you out of decisions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess people say there’s always some tiny possibility with anything, like when the weather man says it won’t rain and then it does, you know? But no, not really. Not with this.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working this family business in a way that puts her closer to the money and leaves me out of the decisions. Folks keep trying to smooth it over, saying I’m taking it wrong, but I know what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on too long for me to act like it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen or anything your husband's sister could do that would change your mind about her trying to push you out of decisions?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t know what she could do at this point. She could say all kinds of nice things, but people say nice things when they’re trying to keep things smooth, too.\n\nIf she really stepped back from the money and the business decisions, maybe… but then I’d still have to watch, because that’s how these things go. My aunt used to say, “Don’t just listen to what people say, watch where they put their hands,” and that’s true with money especially.\n\nI came here because my husband’s sister has been working herself into the family business so she can get control and push me out. Folks keep trying to smooth it over, but I know what I’ve been seeing, and it’s been going on too long for me to just change my mind because she smiles at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's sister would admit to trying to push you out of decisions if she actually was?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think she would admit it. People don’t usually come right out and say, “Yes, I’m trying to push you out,” especially when there’s money involved.\n\nShe’d probably make it sound like she’s just helping, or keeping things organized. That’s what makes it hard, you know? My mother used to say you can tell more by what folks do than what they say, and I’ve seen enough over this past year with the family business. She’s been getting herself closer to the money and the decisions, and then everyone wants to smooth it over like I’m just misunderstanding. But I know what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people in your family see your husband's sister's actions the same way you do?\n\nPatient: Some of them act like they don’t, or they say I’m making too much of it. My husband especially wants everybody to get along, you know, like if you just sit down at Sunday dinner and pass the potatoes then everything is fine. That’s how his family is, they don’t like things said out loud.\n\nBut I came because his sister has been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of the decisions. It’s been more than a year of little things adding up. Folks try to smooth it over, make it sound like she’s just helping, but I know what I’ve been seeing. Whether they admit they see it or not, that’s another thing."
